**[00:37:18]** is extremely evil

**[00:37:20]** That's why they were slaughtered

**[00:37:21]** Jesus, the Savior, was the Savior

**[00:37:23]** The Son of God being sacrificed is the highest good in history

**[00:37:27]** What is the difference between those situations?

**[00:37:29]** Atheists and skeptics will say

**[00:37:32]** So you look at the hypocrisy

**[00:37:34]** God is judging these people for sacrificing their babies

**[00:37:36]** and yet He sacrifices His Son

**[00:37:38]** Doesn't make sense

**[00:37:39]** Well, it doesn't make sense because you don't understand God's righteousness

**[00:37:42]** You don't understand the absoluteness of God

**[00:37:45]** You can't compare Canaanites to God

**[00:37:47]** God is the source of life

**[00:37:48]** He is the originator

**[00:37:50]** So things are very different for Him than they are for people

**[00:37:52]** So God's involvement is what makes those two situations different

**[00:37:58]** Jesus was God

**[00:38:00]** And He was also being sacrificed to redeem God's name

**[00:38:02]** Very different sacrifice

**[00:38:04]** Whereas children being sacrificed and burned to Moloch

**[00:38:07]** is self-destruction

**[00:38:09]** It's evil

**[00:38:11]** So God is what makes those two situations different

**[00:38:14]** That's what is to remember

**[00:38:16]** That God is the source of all good

**[00:38:18]** And without understanding how God fits into the picture

**[00:38:21]** We can't be presumptuous and say

**[00:38:24]** Well, this is good or this is evil

**[00:38:26]** How do you know?

**[00:38:27]** How do you really know?

**[00:38:29]** We can recognize evil

**[00:38:31]** You know, the thing that's been happening in Gaza is evil

**[00:38:34]** We know that objectively

**[00:38:36]** However, you can't stop there

**[00:38:39]** You gotta remember that God is sovereign and using all things for the good

**[00:38:42]** And we don't know fully all these situations that happen

**[00:38:46]** What God is using them for

**[00:38:47]** But we can trust because we know God's character

**[00:38:51]** Now it's important to also review the idea of fate versus biblical predestination

**[00:38:56]** The biggest issue with predestination and evil that people have

**[00:39:00]** Is that they confuse predestination, biblical predestination with fate

**[00:39:04]** Which is a pagan notion

**[00:39:07]** Fate is a pagan notion

**[00:39:09]** It's a mechanistic view of the universe where things are predetermined for the hell of being predetermined

**[00:39:14]** There's no purpose, there's no glorifying person

**[00:39:17]** There's no anchor

**[00:39:19]** There's just predetermined events

**[00:39:21]** Of course, fate is not true

**[00:39:23]** But if it were true, then yeah, it would be better not to be born in such a system

**[00:39:26]** Because things are, you know, good, bad, luck of the draw, random

**[00:39:30]** For no reason

**[00:39:32]** There's no inherent purpose to any of the predetermined events

**[00:39:35]** Whereas biblical predestination is anchored in a personal God

**[00:39:38]** Who is pursuing his glory and is absolutely 100% capable of achieving his outcome

**[00:39:44]** That means that biblical predestination says that everything that happens is predestined

**[00:39:49]** Has the highest possible chance of success in the world

**[00:39:51]** And that's the only possible good attached to it

**[00:39:54]** Even when that purpose is not immediately available in the sense that it's visible or understandable

**[00:40:00]** We can trust that everything that is predestined is predestined for the highest possible good there is

**[00:40:07]** And that is good news

**[00:40:09]** Fate, which is pagan, is bad news

**[00:40:11]** That's why people reject biblical predestination

**[00:40:13]** Because they confuse it with fate

**[00:40:14]** And say, oh, I don't want to be predetermined

**[00:40:16]** God is just forcing me to do things

**[00:40:18]** Well, that's not how predestination works, my friends

**[00:40:20]** Predestination is, again, you have primary and secondary causes

**[00:40:23]** God acts in history and he chooses not to act

**[00:40:26]** And as a result of his actions, certain things happen and certain things don't

**[00:40:29]** He can either restrain sin or he doesn't restrain sin

**[00:40:31]** And so he's the primary cause

**[00:40:33]** And nevertheless, whether he restrains or not doesn't matter

**[00:40:36]** In the long view of history because everything is anchored in his glory

**[00:40:40]** Meaning everything that happens is incredibly purposeful

**[00:40:44]** When you study history, you study the movements of God through time

**[00:40:47]** And when he chose to restrain, when he didn't

**[00:40:49]** When he chose to act, when he chose not to act

**[00:40:51]** And as a result, things happened, you know, one way or another

**[00:40:54]** And they're all either or for God's glory

**[00:40:58]** So biblical predestination is anchored in God's glory and his pursuit of his glory

**[00:41:02]** God's glory is the most valuable, precious, good thing there is

**[00:41:06]** And it is the central theme of the Bible

**[00:41:08]** If we do not learn to see God's glory as the end of all things

**[00:41:13]** Then we're going to stumble into man-centered theology

**[00:41:17]** Which, unfortunately, has conquered us

**[00:41:19]** Conquered the minds of many
